That was the one that mattered most: nothing in the CLI or the API calls `prune()` (it runs every 50th `store()`), so the log line is its only channel. `traces.py:146` warns once per process then logs at debug with a running `unexported` count, naming the exporters that received nothing — a per-trace warning on a CLI one-shot would have been the swallow plus noise. `reindex.py:376` warns with the alias, collection, unparseable stamp and expected format before skipping, so a stamp-format change announces that retention has stopped rather than silently stopping it. `cli/commands/pipeline.py:131` keeps absorbing the cancellation — **that judgement stands**, since the command cancelled the follower itself and rethrowing would let a progress-printer's shutdown replace a finished job's outcome — and now leaves a debug line carrying the job id. **Residual, deliberately unchanged**: that handler also absorbs a `CancelledError` from an *outer* cancellation of the command's own task, indistinguishable here without `asyncio.current_task().cancelling()`. **Both `unjustified-ignore` sites were removed rather than annotated**, since an annotated ignore is still an ignore: `check_package.py:41` dropped a `**kwargs: object` no caller ever used, which was the sole reason `subprocess.run`'s overloads could not resolve, and `pipeline.py:444` replaced a `frozenset` plus ignore-as-cast with a `Mapping[str, Distance]` lookup so the literal is narrowed by the table instead of asserted over argparse's `Any`. Eleven tests added, six in a new `tests/unit/services/test_traces.py` that had no unit module at all, each with an opposite-polarity pin so no fix can decay into unconditional noise. **Every pin was proved by reverting its handler to the old swallowing form: all six failed, and passed again on restore.** Because an empty map makes `test_every_ratchet_entry_still_describes_the_real_tree` vacuous, `test_the_real_tree_carries_no_error_severity_finding_at_all` now asserts zero error-severity findings — otherwise "ratchet empty" would be indistinguishable from "nothing measured", the T12 defect in miniature. Gate exits 0 with 15 unchanged warnings (7 `swallow-documented`, 7 `broad-except`, 1 `untyped-raise`). **Still open: the `Typed error taxonomy holds` step in ci.yml**, which no agent could add while another held the workflow files, so the gate has still never run in CI. - [x] TASK-0318: **Implement `Idempotency-Key` as a header across every mutating endpoint.** Today only `POST /v1/ingest` is idempotent, through an `idempotency_key` **body field** (`api/schemas.py:53`) resolved by `Journal.find_by_idempotency_key`; collection create and delete, DLQ retry, the admin routes and replay all perform duplicate work on a replay. **Both documents were wrong about this, in opposite directions, since the API was specified** — `api-reference.md` claimed every mutating endpoint accepts an `Idempotency-Key` header, which no endpoint reads, while `reliability.md` called the whole feature unimplemented, when ingest has shipped it as a body field all along. Both corrected 2026-11-07, and the body field documented in the ingest request table for the first time. A reliability doc that understates what ships is the rarer failure and the more wasteful one: it invites somebody to rebuild working code. **Closed.** `Idempotency-Key` ships on all 9 mutating operations as ASGI middleware (`api/idempotency.py`) over a durable store (`services/idempotency.py`), so a mutating router added later is covered the moment it is mounted — a per-route dependency's failure mode is forgetting one, which is how this gap persisted. **The inventory is the finding**: 13 mutating operations existed and exactly one had any replay protection, and that one deduped only the job while comparing nothing else. Scope is `(tenant, key)` over a length-prefix hash of method/path/query/body; replay returns the original response with `Idempotent-Replayed: true`; a changed request under a live key is `422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`; a concurrent duplicate loses an `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` race and gets `409 IDEMPOTENCY_IN_PROGRESS` + `Retry-After: 1` rather than executing. Only 2xx is stored, so a 503 stays retryable under the same key. A 15-minute lease frees a key whose worker died, instead of holding it for the full 24 h and forcing the client to invent a new key — the duplicate the feature exists to prevent. `/v1/query`, `/v1/retrieve`, `/v1/estimate` and `/v1/replay` are excluded and documented as POST-for-the-body rather than POST-for-an-effect; a key sent to one is ignored, not refused. The `idempotency_key` body field still works, is deprecated for 1.0, and announces itself via `Deprecation: true` plus an OpenAPI flag. **Found and fixed en route:** `Journal.find_by_idempotency_key` matched on the key alone while `create_job` had recorded the tenant, so under multi-tenancy one tenant replaying another's key received that tenant's job id and had its own ingest silently dropped — a job-id disclosure and a lost corpus from one collision on a client-chosen string. **Shipped without a config flag**, deviating from CLAUDE.md's default-false rule with the argument that the middleware is inert unless a client sends the header and that defaulting it off would make the newly documented contract untrue; `IdempotencyStore(enabled=...)` exists so a flag is one line (TASK-0356). 22 tests. **Unverified:** the multi-worker claim is reasoned from the on-disk model and the `O_EXCL` primitive, not measured with real Gunicorn workers; the concurrency proof is two coroutines in one event loop. — ✅ 2026-11-08

Separately and unrelated to that decision: **66 non-marker comment lines across 17 files under `src/` break the docstrings-only rule**, several of them sitting inside the very exception handlers TASK-0317 lists, and **no check exists for this at all**. `services/autopilot.py:498` shows the correct form. Excludes `# noqa`, `# type: ignore` and `# pragma:`. **Closed, and both prior counts were wrong.** The comment gate now exists: `scripts/check_comments.py` + 26 tests, wired into `ci.yml`'s quality job as "Only permitted comments exist". It reads `tokenize` output so a `#` in a string literal never matches, reports contiguous lines as one finding, and ratchets by **path** — line numbers moved 13 lines inside `api/auth.py` between two scans minutes apart. **Three vacuity guards, each tested**: zero files, zero comment tokens, and zero markers recognised anywhere, the last because the marker pattern is the whole rule. **The re-measure**: not 66 lines across 17 files, but **130 non-marker lines in 53 blocks across 30 of `src/`'s 151 modules**, plus 33 lines in 14 blocks across 7 of `scripts/`'s 13 files — and every one standalone, not a single trailing inline comment in either tree. All 157 lines moved **verbatim** into `docs/code-notes.md`; nothing was deleted and nothing folded into a docstring, since re-measuring 36 docstrings against the four-line cap under nine concurrent agents was the riskier half. Removal was proved by re-parsing each file and refusing any write where `ast.dump` changed; the orchestrator re-verified 30 of 49 changed files as AST-identical to HEAD, the other 19 being files other agents were editing concurrently. **The `tests/` decision: do not widen `IGNORE_SCAN_ROOTS`, and the numbers are why.** `tests/` holds 141 `# type: ignore` in 31 files, 135 unjustified, against zero in `src/` and `scripts/`; of the 135, **102 (76%) are `[arg-type]` on deliberate stubs** — one idiom repeated, so widening buys 135 copies of the same sentence and teaches readers to skim past the marker, costing the gate its meaning where it works. Two residuals that are not that idiom are TASK-0344. Measured 0.37-0.39 s locally on Windows; **never run on a Linux runner**. — ✅ 2026-11-08
- [x] TASK-0320: **Three write paths still bypass the keyword-leg preflight.** TASK-0119 wired `require_keyword_leg` into `POST /v1/ingest`, its retry-DLQ sibling and `fasterrag index create` on 2026-11-07, but `cli/commands/pipeline.py:run_ingest`, `services/ingestion.py:IngestionService.accept` and `api/collections.py:create_collection` still write without it — so a CLI ingest, a direct library ingest, or an API collection create over a dense-only collection with `retrieval.hybrid: true` still fails the old way, on Qdrant with `EMBED_PROVIDER_ERROR` naming a point id. **`IngestionService.accept` is the better home than `run_ingest`**, since it covers CLI, REST and direct library callers in one place, where `run_ingest` would additionally need to build and close its own adapter. `POST /v1/collections` is the worst of the three today: it reports `"sparse": settings.retrieval.hybrid` as fact when Qdrant added nothing, so it does not merely fail later — it affirms something untrue. **A residual hole neither this nor TASK-0305 would close**: Qdrant's `_describe` computes `sparse = bool(info.config.params.sparse_vectors)`, true for *any* sparse config rather than specifically the `bm25` named vector, so a collection carrying a foreign sparse vector passes the preflight and still fails at upsert. Narrow but real, and it lives in the adapter. Found 2026-11-07 while wiring TASK-0119. — ✅ 2026-11-08 (**closed in two places, not three.** The gate went into `IngestionService.accept` **before `journal.create_job`**, so a refused job leaves no record — a queued job nobody will settle is worse than no job — and `run_ingest` needs no check of its own: both its branches reach the pipeline through `accept`, directly under `--watch` and via `ingest()` otherwise, inside the `except FasterRagError` that already renders a typed failure. **`POST /v1/collections` was the worst of the three** and is fixed at the root: it now re-reads the collection after creating and returns `"sparse": audit.present` instead of `"sparse": settings.retrieval.hybrid`, so the 201 describes the collection rather than echoing the request — which also fixes the inverse case, a hybrid collection reported dense-only under `hybrid: false`. One metadata round trip **per job**, pinned by a test counting listings, and deliberately not on the per-document path where the same probe would tax every document for a mistake made once. 13 tests. **A fake was lying in exactly the way the production bug did**: `RecordingAdapter.list_collections` drops `spec.sparse` and always reports `sparse=False`, which broke two pre-existing tests the moment the gate became real; it was subclassed rather than edited, since it is shared. **Left deliberately**: `api/ingest.py` now probes twice per REST job, because its tests stub the service and removing the route's call would silently drop the REST gate from the suite's view. **The residual hole stands** — Qdrant's `_describe` reports any sparse config as the keyword leg, so a foreign sparse vector passes both gates and still fails at upsert (TASK-0305). **Fakes only**: no Docker daemon, and the integration module needs moving to `tests/integration/` to reach the fixture that starts Qdrant (TASK-0322))
- [x] TASK-0321: **Harden `scripts/mutation_run.py` so a pathological mutant is a recorded verdict rather than a dead runner.** Split from TASK-0316 ✅, whose investigation could not touch the script because three mutation campaigns were live against it. Four changes, highest value first. (1) **A flushed progress line per mutant** in `_run_target` naming the mutant in flight — without it `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` has nothing to act on, because `main()` writes nothing to stdout until every mutant has run, and the next SIGTERM is again anonymous. (2) **Bound the child's memory**: a `--memory-mb` option wiring `resource.setrlimit(RLIMIT_AS)` through `preexec_fn` in `_pytest`'s `subprocess.run`, off by default on Windows where `resource` does not exist. A runaway mutant then dies with a `MemoryError` inside its own pytest run, is recorded as killed, and the campaign continues — instead of taking the runner with it. That conversion is the whole point. (3) **Record peak child RSS** from `getrusage(RUSAGE_CHILDREN)` into `RunReport.as_dict()`, so the artifact says how close a run got. (4) **Write `--json-out` incrementally per target**, so a killed run leaves the targets it finished rather than nothing. Also observed and worth a decision: `result.timed_out += 1; result.killed += 1` counts a hung mutant as killed, which absorbs a time or memory pathology into a good-looking number instead of surfacing it. **Closed, all four changes plus the counting decision.** (1) `_progress()` writes an explicitly flushed stdout line per mutant *before* it runs, naming file, line, operator and substitution — the flush is in the script rather than left to `PYTHONUNBUFFERED`, so the last line before a SIGTERM names the mutant in flight. (2) `--memory-mb N` caps the child's address space via `RLIMIT_AS` through `preexec_fn`; `resource` is imported by name so Windows gets no cap, and a cap *asked for* where `resource` is absent exits 2 rather than running uncapped. **The cap binds the baseline run too**, so a cap too low for a target's own suite surfaces as `not-run` instead of a free perfect score. (3) `peak_child_rss_bytes` from `getrusage(RUSAGE_CHILDREN)`, KiB-scaled except on darwin, `null` off POSIX. (4) `--json-out` is rewritten after every target through a `.partial` + `os.replace`, so a kill leaves the finished targets whole. **Decision: a timeout stays inside `killed` and is now named.** Keeping it preserves `executed == killed + survived`, the only arithmetic anyone does with this file, and calling a timeout a survivor would publish "no test defends this line" about a line whose test never finished — a false claim of exactly the kind this runner exists to prevent. What was wrong was the anonymity, so every timed-out mutant is now listed like a survivor in JSON, terminal and job summary; the "214 kills of which 4 were timeouts" case now says which 4. No machine consumer of the artifact exists (grep: only the nightly's exit-code `case`), and no key changed meaning. **Executed on a real Linux kernel rather than argued**: under WSL Ubuntu-24.04 a 512 MiB child capped at 64 MiB dies `MemoryError` rc 1 while the identical uncapped child exits 0, and a 9-mutant campaign at `--memory-mb 512` killed both runaway mutants and continued, reporting 503 MiB peak against the cap. **Unverified:** the hosted runner, where a cgroup OOM kill arrives without `RLIMIT_AS` being hit, and a calibrated cap for the six real targets. 20 new tests. — ✅ 2026-11-08
- [x] TASK-0322: **The unit suite is not deterministic, and nobody knows why.** Found 2026-11-08 while re-deriving release readiness. Two full runs forty minutes apart with `-p no:randomly` gave **6 failed / 3,340 passed** and then **17 failed / 3,194 passed** — *a different failing set each time*. Every failing module passed in isolation (`test_doctor.py` 33 passed; `test_live_providers.py` 4 passed, 9 skipped), and a targeted environment-leak probe came back clean. The obvious explanation is that eight agents were editing `src/` during both runs (`git status` went 6 → 29 paths mid-session), and that is probably it — but **it is not established**, and the alternative, cross-test state leakage, is the kind of defect that makes every other measurement in this repository untrustworthy. One instance is already understood: four `test_doctor.py` failures on 2026-11-07 were a port 8000 bind held by a parallel agent, environmental and real. **This needs a re-run on a frozen tree with no agents running**, and if it reproduces there it is the highest-priority correctness task in the ledger, because a flaky suite cannot gate a release. Also unresolved from the same session: two `tests/eval/test_live_providers.py` OpenAI cases fail in a full run and skip in isolation, which means a credential is reaching them in one context and not the other. **Resolved as not a suite defect.** Six consecutive runs of `tests/unit` on a frozen git worktree at `0c7f81a`, with no agents touching it, gave an identical result every time: **3399 passed, 13 skipped, 1 xfailed, 0 failed** (3413 collected). Runs 1 and 3 were byte-identical after normalising the duration. The 6-failed/3,340-passed then 17-failed/3,194-passed divergence is attributed to eight agents editing `src/` during both runs; the 135-test gap in *collected* totals is the signature of modules failing to import mid-rewrite. **`-p no:randomly` was a no-op throughout**: `pytest-randomly` is not installed and pytest accepts `-p no:<unknown>` silently, so the suite has always run in fixed collection order and cross-test order dependence remains **unproven in either direction** (TASK-0354). **The investigation nearly measured the wrong tree**: the venv's editable install writes an absolute `.pth` pointing at the main checkout, so a bare `import fasterrag` from a worktree resolves to the tree nine agents were editing; `pythonpath = ["src", "scripts"]` in `pyproject.toml` is what prevented it, and the guard was run first and the import path confirmed. Prevented from recurring by a provenance header in `tests/conftest.py` recording commit SHA, uncommitted-path count and the resolved `fasterrag` import path into every run's output — **including under `-q`**, which suppresses the normal header and is the flag every measurement in this repo uses. **The port-8000 incident does not reproduce at this commit**: holding `127.0.0.1:8000` and running all 46 doctor tests gives 46 passed. **A second, independent source of count drift was found** and is closed by accident: a unit test shelled out to the Docker daemon and, with a Qdrant container up, created and wrote a real collection, so the unit tier's pass/skip counts were a function of machine state rather than the commit — TASK-0324 moved that file to `tests/integration/` the same day. — ✅ 2026-11-08
- [x] TASK-0323: **The nightly still measures only parse-and-chunk, and declares a backend it never reaches.** TASK-0314 ✅ made the `measure` job emit a real document by running `--suite ingest`, but the **query** suite — the one with the latency percentiles, the one `docs/performance.md` is about — still does not run, because it calls the configured LLM and a hosted runner has no paid credential (`ollama` is the only offline provider and would need a model download; not evaluated). The Qdrant service was added as that task directed and is **not contacted** under `--suite ingest`, which is stated in the workflow comment rather than left for someone to discover. Either supply a credential and restore the query suite, or delete the service and record that the nightly measures ingestion only — but do not leave a declared dependency nothing uses, because an unexplained service is how 83 days of measuring nothing went unnoticed in the first place. **Closed. The Qdrant service is deleted and the workflow states what it measures.** The `measure` job runs the ingest suite only — parse and chunk over `tests/eval/datasets/handbook/corpus`, no retrieval, embedding, indexing or generation — verified by reading `_ingest_suite`, which constructs no vector-db adapter. The credential half is the maintainer's and is priced in [performance.md](performance.md) §3.7. **Ollama evaluated from vendor-published figures, none measured on a runner**: the v0.40.15 tarball is 1,543,547,172 bytes, the container is *worse* at 3,392,421,545 compressed, and the smallest usable weights are `smollm2:135m` 271 MB / `qwen2.5:0.5b` 398 MB. Weights cache cleanly inside the 10 GB budget with 7-day eviction, but the **recommendation is against it** for a latency series: a 0.5B model on a 4-core shared runner measures CPU token throughput, and those numbers would carry the suite name `query` while measuring something else. **The audit found five more ways this file could go green without measuring, four now closed**: the redirect left a zero-byte file that read as an output (new assertion step, no `continue-on-error`, fails on missing/empty/unparseable/wrong-suite/no-median); both uploads defaulted to `if-no-files-found: warn`, which would leave a green run with no artifact *and* rob the next night of its baseline (now `error`); `pytest -m eval` exits 0 when every test skips (now `-rs`, legible but not yet impossible — TASK-0349); and the comparison reported an unhandled traceback as "a metric regressed". Added `timeout-minutes: 30` and a non-cancelling `concurrency` group. Both `continue-on-error` flags survive the audit, each contained by a step that fails on the same evidence, each comment now naming that step. **Empirically**: dispatched run **42419161973** proved the TASK-0314 fix emits a real document (`ingest | 6 documents, 632 tokens | p50 3.05 ms, p95 3.21, throughput 325.112/s`) and went red correctly on the previous night's empty baseline, and confirmed from scheduled run 42359949403 that `measure` concluded **success** on 2026-11-07 having written a zero-byte document — the 83-day defect was live until this landed. The `mutation` job also succeeded for the first time ever in that run. **The edited file has never run on a runner**; verification is `yaml.safe_load`, `bash -n` over all 38 run steps, and the new step scripts exercised against the real artifact plus four failure states. — ✅ 2026-11-08
- [x] TASK-0324: **`require_keyword_leg` cannot separate the lookup name from the displayed one, so a multi-tenant refusal leaks the backend-prefixed collection.** `api/collections.py` must pass `scoped_name(body.name, tenant)` for the audit to be about the right collection, so the 409 detail names `'acme__docs'` — the one thing that module's docstring says never reaches a response. Single-tenant is unaffected, since `scoped_name(name, None) == name`. Fix is a `display: str | None = None` parameter on `audit_keyword_leg`/`require_keyword_leg` feeding `KeywordLegAudit.name`; the agent that found it declined to copy the implementation to work around it. Separately: `tests/unit/services/test_keyword_leg_write_paths.py` holds two `integration`-marked cases that skip unconditionally because the session `qdrant` fixture lives in `tests/integration/conftest.py` and is unreachable from `tests/unit/`. They belong in `tests/integration/`; they were left rather than given their own provisioning block, since a second never-executed place that creates containers is a second place that leaks them. Both found 2026-11-08 closing TASK-0320. **Closed, and the fix was one field wider than the diagnosis.** Feeding `display` into `KeywordLegAudit.name` alone would have moved the prefix rather than removed it: `detail` interpolates `live` too, the physical collection read back from the backend, which is `'acme__docs'` whatever `name` holds. `live` is therefore also rendered under the caller's naming, keeping the suffix that distinguishes a green build (`acme__docs-v2026…` → `docs-v2026…`) and replacing only the part the caller never chose. Single-tenant output is byte-identical, which is why no existing test moved; the warning log still carries the real backend name, because a log is not a response. **Pinned on the response body, not the helper** — a unit test of `require_keyword_leg` is precisely what let this through, since the audit is the one place the scoped name is correct. A second test guards the obvious wrong fix: auditing `body.name` to hide the prefix would make the preflight examine a collection nobody created, so a tenant create over a dense-only unscoped `docs` must still return 201. **The sibling sweep checked every API error detail and response body** — `list`/`get`/`delete` collections, both ingest paths, the sources guards, the unauthenticated `/readyz` (whose dependency details the Qdrant adapter already strips of host and vendor text), the three problem handlers, traces, auth refusals and metrics labels — all clean, and **found two more that are not**: TASK-0340 and TASK-0339. `tests/unit/services/test_keyword_leg_write_paths.py` **moved to `tests/integration/`**, reusing the session `qdrant` fixture rather than gaining a second provisioning block; both cases are now collected in the integration run (369 collected, shown) instead of being unreachable from the unit tree, and they still skip for want of a Docker daemon — through the shared fixture, which is the difference. That move also closes a source of unit-tier count drift found independently under TASK-0322. — ✅ 2026-11-08

Worse: relaxing the tenant comparison from `!=` to a prefix test let the key bound to `acme` act for **`acme-corp`** with the whole suite green — a cross-tenant read with no signal anywhere. - [ ] TASK-0326: (security, maintainer decision) **Nothing throttles an unauthenticated or wrong-key request against the control plane.** `AuthMiddleware` reaches `RateLimiter.allow(key.secret)` only *after* `KeyRegistry.resolve` succeeds, and `api/main.py` mounts no limiter in front of it — metrics, correlation-id, auth and body-limit only — so online guessing of an API key is bounded by the network alone. `load_keys` imposes no minimum length or entropy either: a one-character key loads fine. `docs/security.md` §2 explains why the limiter is keyed per-key rather than per-IP (“a limit keyed on something the caller chooses is not a limit”) but never states this cost, so the gap reads as considered when it is merely unmentioned. **This library is going to public PyPI and operators will run it on the open internet.** Decide between throttling refused credentials per source address, requiring a minimum key length, and documenting the exposure explicitly — and record which. Found 2026-11-08 by mutation-testing `api/auth.py`. **All five claims verified 2026-11-08, by measurement not only by reading**: at `rate_limit_per_minute=5`, 400 refused requests (200 wrong-key, 200 no-credential) returned 401 with **zero 429s**, while the same correct key got 5x200 then 15x429 — the limiter works and is simply never consulted for a refused credential. A one-character key loads, authenticates and is rate-limited normally, and `fasterrag doctor` checks key strength nowhere. **Two costs the entry did not name**: every refusal logs a WARNING, so an unauthenticated flood is also log amplification (400 attempts, 400 lines); and a refused request produces **no metric at all**, because auth runs before `MetricsMiddleware` by design, so guessing is invisible outside the log stream and there is nothing to alert on. **There is no trusted-proxy handling anywhere in `src/`** — zero hits for `x-forwarded`, `proxy_headers`, `scope["client"]` — while `docs/deployment.md` makes a TLS-terminating reverse proxy the documented topology, so per-IP throttling would either bucket every caller as one (a self-inflicted DoS) or key on caller-controlled `X-Forwarded-For`, the very thing §2 already rejects. **Recommendation on file**: minimum key length + document the exposure + add a refused-auth counter now; per-source throttling later, opt-in, behind `security.trusted_proxies`. The magnitude of this exposure is entirely a function of key entropy, which is the one variable the code does not constrain today.
- [x] TASK-0327: (security) **`_parse_scopes` fails open on a present-but-empty scope field, granting `admin`.** `frozenset(scopes or ALL_SCOPES)` means an entry of the form `k:`, `k::acme` or `k: , ` receives **all four scopes including `admin`** — confirmed directly 2026-11-08: `_parse_scopes('')`, `' '` and `' , '` each return `['admin', 'collections', 'ingest', 'query']`. `docs/security.md` §2 documents only the *bare* secret case, and the documented grammar `<secret>[:<scopes>[:<tenant>]]` **gives an operator no other way to attach a tenant without naming scopes**, so writing `k::acme` silently produces a full admin key. The failure direction is toward more privilege, which is the opposite of how `required_scope` treats omission. Decide whether an explicitly empty scope field should be a `ConfigError`, and document the answer either way. Sibling of TASK-0295, which is the same shape one field over. **Closed by failing the blank scope field and giving the grammar the spelling it lacked.** `ALL_SCOPES_TOKEN` (`*`) now means all four in writing, so `k:*:acme` says "default scopes, this tenant" and `k::acme` is a startup `ConfigError` naming the 1-based entry and the fix. The two halves are inseparable: failing `k::acme` closed without a replacement spelling would have traded a silent privilege escalation for a hard startup failure with no documented remedy. **A bare secret is untouched** — `entry.partition(":")` returns the separator, so an omitted field and a blank field are now different things, information the old code discarded. `*` is a union, not an override, so `*,collection` still errors on the typo. Verified directly: `k` → all four/no tenant; `k::acme` → ConfigError; `k:*:acme` → all four/tenant acme; `good:query;bad::acme` → ConfigError naming entry 2. 15 cases pinned; `docs/security.md` §2 carries the decision table and three parser edges. **TASK-0295 is deliberately not covered**: it is the same shape one field over, but its grant is documented, warned about at startup and has no non-breaking replacement, so closing it invalidates every key file. Note for whoever takes it that `*` is now a token in this grammar and the obvious any-tenant marker, but `k:query:*` today means the literal tenant id `*` — that reservation has to be made consciously. — ✅ 2026-11-08

- [ ] TASK-0329: **`checksums.sha256` does not cover `manifest.json`, so the manifest is unauthenticated — and the manifest is what the vector-copy compatibility guard reads.** `docs/archive-format.md` line 12 promises “SHA-256 of **every other file**”; `services/archive.py:313` digests only `documents.jsonl`, `chunks.jsonl`, `vectors.jsonl` and `index.lock`, then writes `manifest.json` *after* the checksums member, so it cannot be covered. **Verified by reading the writer 2026-11-08**, and demonstrated end to end by the agent that found it: a real archive whose manifest was rewritten to `model="a-completely-different-model", model_version="999"` and repacked without touching a single checksummed member **passed `open_archive`'s verification and then passed `require_vector_copy_compatible`**, permitting a vector copy into a different embedding space. **No other guard catches it**: the counts, width and referential-integrity checks all compare the manifest against the rows, and `model`/`model_version` have no row to disagree with. That is precisely the “answers every query confidently and wrongly” failure the module's own docstring exists to prevent, and it is reachable by anyone who can hand an operator a `.fragx`. **Mutation testing cannot find this** — it is a missing entry in a list, not a flipped operator, which is worth recording about the method as well as the bug. The fix is a format change (digest the manifest, or sign it) and needs maintainer authorization; it also wants a `format_version` decision, since an older reader would not verify the new member.
- [x] TASK-0330: **The D11 portability archive layer had never had a mutant run against it, and 62 of 200 survived at near-total line coverage.** Measured 2026-11-08 with the committed runner at `--seed 1707 --timeout 120` and **no budget**, so **200 of 200 generated mutants executed and no sampling occurred**: `archive.py` 34 killed / 21 survived, `archive_import.py` 105 / 40, in 22m05s. **One reported kill proved flaky and the baseline is corrected down to 138 killed / 62 survived** — `archive_import.py:1006:67` survives deterministically when re-probed against byte-identical HEAD test files, and the flake traces to the two `tracemalloc` `peak < payload // 4` assertions, the only non-deterministic ones in the suite, on a machine shared with three agents. **The survivors clustered exactly where a silent wrong answer costs most.** Both decompression-bomb ceilings were unpinned **because every bomb test monkeypatches them down first, so the suite passed at values that bound nothing**; both bomb boundaries were off-by-one-swappable; the manifest-counts sentinel `-1` could become `0`, letting an archive that *omits* `counts.vectors` read as one declaring zero; `sort_keys`, `ensure_ascii` and every tar header field were free, so the byte-identical-export guarantee held only for extracted member bytes and not for the `.fragx` file itself; the archived-width-outranks-configured and lockfile-outranks-config rules were both swappable; a chunk's real `span` could be replaced by `{0,0}` on restore, producing citations that look perfectly well formed; a short provider response silently truncated an upsert batch and reported success; and **all seven `retryable=False` flags on the refusal paths were unpinned**, so a `CONFLICT` that can never succeed could be marked retryable and drive a client to retry forever. Closed by 31 new tests and 5 strengthened, each hand-verified by breaking its line in an isolated copy of `src/`. Re-measured, same seed, same full set: **200/200 executed, 186 killed, 14 survived, 0 timed out** — 48 closed. The 14 remaining are **9 proved equivalent** (`strict=True` in `sparse_record` is unreachable because `SparseVector.__post_init__` already rejects unequal legs; `ImportCounts.documents`'s default is dead; `_write_lockfiles`'s `mkdir(parents=True)` is redundant because `LockStore.write` already created the root; and six dataclass decorator arguments with no observable effect **on any path these modules expose**, stated that way rather than as general equivalence) and **5 deliberately open and argued**: `batch_size: int = 64` and `_digest`'s block size are undocumented tuning knobs whose real properties are pinned instead; `supported_major`'s `except (AttributeError, IndexError)` is **dead at every typed call site** and killable only by a `mypy`-forbidden call; and one log-only mutant that drops the row counts from the “verified a portable archive” line, left as the cheapest next pickup. Found 2026-11-08: the first archive baseline came back `not-run` on both targets because `test_archive_format_spec.py` reads `docs/archive-format.md` through `parents[3]`. Two findings beyond the instance are TASK-0360 and TASK-0361. **The two `tracemalloc` assertions** now assert `peak < payload // 4 + 2 MiB` and re-measure up to three times keeping the best, at zero cost when the first passes. Measured before changing anything, 3 repeats each: dense 1,555,503 / 1,554,953 / 1,555,017 B against an 18,082,113 B payload; sparse 1,584,868 / 1,585,004 / 1,584,820 B against 20,974,113 B — **spread under 600 bytes, so the loaded-machine flip was not reproduced and its cause is not claimed**. - [x] TASK-0333: **`ModelTokenCounter.count` swallows an encode failure with no log, and the new taxonomy gate files it under the category it told us to ignore.** `core/chunking/tokenizer.py:81` catches `Exception` and returns the estimate in silence, while the sibling handler at line 48 warns with the model and error type. A tokenizer that loads but cannot encode therefore degrades an entire corpus from real token counts to the 4-chars-per-token estimate — **changing every chunk boundary and so every chunk id** — with nothing in the logs. CLAUDE.md requires every caught exception to be logged with a correlation id or rethrown. **The gate finding is the more useful half**: `check_error_taxonomy.py` does flag this line, but only as `broad-except`, the rule its own author measured at **7 of 7 false positives** and recommended never promoting to blocking — so a genuine silent swallow is hiding inside the bucket judged safe to ignore. The blocking `swallow` rule misses it because the handler body is a substantive `return self._estimate.count(stripped)` rather than `pass`/`return None`. **A handler that returns a fallback value without logging is a silent swallow in substance**, and is probably the commonest form; extending the blocking rule to cover it, and re-measuring the false-positive rate on the real tree, is the task. Found 2026-11-08 while closing the chunking mutation slice. **Closed, and the gate half was the more valuable one.** `ModelTokenCounter.count` now warns with `model` and `error` in the same shape as the loader's handler at line 48, **once per counter instance** rather than once per call — `count()` runs once per candidate split, so an unguarded warning would be thousands of lines per document, while a counter is built per document. A new **blocking** rule `swallow-fallback` fires on a handler whose entire body is `return <expr>` for a substantive `expr`, catching `Exception`/`BaseException`, where `expr` does not mention the caught exception; it suppresses `broad-except` on the same handler so a blocking verdict never also sits in a warning bucket, and a new vacuity guard fails the run if the matcher never sees a candidate (30 in `src/` today). **Re-measured on the real tree rather than hoped at**: 34 handlers have a lone fallback `return`; 14 return a value built from the exception (false positives, exempt by construction); of the remaining 20, hand classification gave **7 true positives and 13 false positives — 65%, unshippable as blocking**. The false positives are predicates and probes where the exception *is* the answer (`_is_ip_address`, `supports_pooling`, `port_is_free`, the `csv.Sniffer` fallback) or values that report the failure themselves (`"unknown (git unavailable)"`). Narrowing to a broad catch left **exactly one site — `tokenizer.py:81`, a true positive: 0 false positives of 1** — so it ships blocking with `RATCHET` still `{}`, and the six narrow-catch true positives are TASK-0342. **The "7 of 7 false positives" reading of `broad-except` recorded in this task was wrong**: `tokenizer.py:81` was one of those seven and was a real swallow, so the rate was 6 of 7 — which is why the defect was hiding in the bucket judged safe to ignore. Proved against the actual pre-fix source rather than fixtures: `check()` over `git show HEAD:…/tokenizer.py` reports the finding and `over_ratchet()` returns it, so the gate would have exited 1; the orchestrator reproduced this independently. **Unverified:** no genuine `transformers` encode failure was staged. — ✅ 2026-11-08
- [x] TASK-0334: **`_distance` silently truncates a ragged embedder.** `core/chunking/semantic.py:66` uses `zip(left, right, strict=False)`, so if an embedding adapter ever returns vectors of differing width the cosine distance is computed over the shorter one's prefix and **returned as a plausible number** — no error, and semantic chunk boundaries placed from it. Surfaced 2026-11-08 by the mutant `strict=False → True`, which survives: the suite cannot tell the two apart, and **the mutant is arguably the correct code**. Deliberately left unpinned rather than freezing the current behaviour, which is the right call — a test here would have made the defect permanent. Fix by making the mismatch a typed error, or by documenting the prefix semantics and pinning those. **Closed with a typed error, not documented prefix semantics.** `_distance` raises `EmbedError` (`EMBED_PROVIDER_ERROR`, `retryable=False`) naming both widths. The violated contract is the embedder's, not the chunker's — `ChunkError` is scoped by its own docstring to offsets, overlap and size bounds, and nothing the chunker did was wrong — and every vector-DB adapter already raises `EmbedError(..., retryable=False)` on a dimension mismatch, so the existing precedent was matched rather than a new shape invented. `EMBED_PROVIDER_ERROR` maps to a 503 problem+json, not a generic 500, and the CPU pool dead-letters the document by code. **The raise comes out of `zip(strict=True)` rather than an explicit `len()` compare, deliberately**: an explicit guard would leave the `strict=` kwarg with no observable effect, so the mutant would survive as an *equivalent* mutant — the exact survivor class this work exists to remove. Measured: flipping to `strict=False` fails exactly the three new tests and passes 184 others; restored, 187 pass. Under the old code the mismatched pair scored `0.0` — "identical topics" — which is why it was invisible. **Unverified:** the dead-letter path was read, not executed with a ragged provider. — ✅ 2026-11-08

The old test still passes and should be renamed or folded in rather than left claiming coverage it does not have. - [ ] TASK-0339: (security, maintainer decision) **The retrieval path is not tenant-scoped at all, and two tenancy models are running side by side.** `grep -c tenant src/fasterrag/services/querying.py` returns **0**, and `POST /v1/retrieve` takes no `CurrentTenant` dependency; `/v1/query` takes one but spends it on the semantic-cache key, the cost governor and traces, never on the collection name or a result filter. `scoped_name` appears nowhere in the query or ingest path — only in `/v1/collections` and `/v1/admin`. Meanwhile `workers/indexer.py:58` **does** write `stored["tenant"] = payload.tenant` into every vector payload, and nothing ever filters on it. So a collection created through the API as `acme__docs` is ingested and queried as `docs`, all tenants' chunks share one unscoped collection tagged with a field nobody reads, and a caller holding a valid `acme` key can post `{"collection": "globex__docs"}` to `/v1/retrieve` or simply query the shared `docs`. `docs/security.md` §2 presents collection-name isolation as *the* multi-tenancy mechanism; it covers two routers. **Needs a maintainer decision on which model is intended** before it is treated as a bug: scope the collection name in the ingest and query paths too, or keep one collection and push a mandatory tenant filter into both retrieval legs — the second must be injected below any caller-supplied `filters` so it cannot be overridden, and touches the `adapters/` boundary. **Verified statically only** (four greps, confirmed by the orchestrator 2026-11-08): `tests/unit/api/test_query.py` stubs retrieval out entirely, so no test could have caught this and a stub-based proof would prove nothing. No cross-tenant query has been executed. **Partly addressed 2026-11-09, and the static finding is now an executed one.** `tests/unit/api/test_query_tenancy.py` drives the real router, the real `AuthMiddleware` and a faithful in-memory adapter: with `auth` and `multi_tenancy` on, an `acme` key **retrieved `globex`'s chunk by naming `globex__docs`**, **retrieved both tenants' chunks from the unnamed shared collection**, and had `POST /v1/query` put another tenant's text into the answer **and into `citations`** — so it leaks even to a caller who cannot see raw chunks. The filter each leg received was `None` in all three; the `tenant` payload field `workers/indexer.py` writes is never read. **It is not safe for any unnoticed reason.** **The filter half now ships behind `security.tenant_scoped_retrieval`, default `false`** — one field and one cross-field validator added to `SecuritySettings`, the validator refusing the flag without `auth` and `multi_tenancy`, so fail-fast is **strengthened rather than weakened**. The tenant is injected in `services/querying.py` after `validate_filter`, at the one point a single filter object reaches **both** legs — a router-level injection would have let a later change scope one leg and forget the other. A body naming the `tenant` key is refused `403 TENANT_FORBIDDEN` rather than silently overridden, because overriding is equally safe for the result set but lets a caller probe isolation without ever being told it exists. **Correction to this entry's framing**: it is option (a), name-scoping, that touches data placement and would 404 every existing multi-tenant deployment; option (b) needs **no adapter change**, since `{"tenant": "x"}` is ordinary equality in the documented grammar every adapter already translates. **Two adjacent defects fell out and are fixed**: `chunk_payload` merged caller `metadata` *after* the tenant, so an ingest could relabel its own chunks into another tenant's filtered view — the filter is only as good as the field — and `POST /v1/replay` built generation with no tenant, which also moves that replay's spend onto the tenant's budget. `docs/security.md` **§3** corrected (not §2, which makes no tenancy claim), including a record of the claim it got wrong, and `cookbook.md`'s multi-tenant SaaS recipe, which would otherwise ship a cross-tenant leak as a recommended config. **Still a maintainer decision**: whether the project commits to name scoping everywhere or to one collection with this filter. The flag does not make ingest or query use `scoped_name`, so both models still coexist (TASK-0381). **Not verified**: no real vector database, no performance measurement, and the backfill and payload-index paths are described rather than built (TASK-0377, TASK-0378).

- [x] TASK-0341: **A bare `pytest` made real, billed OpenAI calls — half fixed, the mechanism remains.** `addopts` carried no marker filter, so `pytest` collected 4051 tests including all 13 live-provider probes. `load_settings` defaults to `env_file=Path(".env")`, **relative to the working directory**, and `_require_referenced_env_vars` calls `load_dotenv(..., override=False)`, which writes into the real `os.environ` permanently and is never undone; `settings_from()` (every CLI command) and `create_app()` both take that default. `.env` holds `OPENAI_API_KEY`, so in a full run from the repo root the key entered the environment and `test_live_providers.py::_gate` stopped skipping. Proven offline with dummy values; the chain was re-confirmed independently by the orchestrator 2026-11-08 (variable **names** only, never values). **Both outcomes are defects**: a failure trains people to re-run, a pass spends the maintainer's money from a routine test command. **Fixed 2026-11-08**: `addopts` now carries `-m 'not eval and not load and not soak and not chaos'`, verified to collect 0 live-provider tests while `pytest -m eval` still collects 30, since a command-line `-m` overrides it. **Still open**: `load_settings` mutating the real process environment at all; gating the live probes on an explicit opt-in rather than on a credential merely being present; and `CLAUDE.md`'s commands section, which calls `pytest` "(unit)" — a false statement that is how this stayed invisible. **Closed, and the side effect turned out to be load-bearing.** Every credential in the system is resolved by a *later* `os.environ` read — `adapters/llm/base.py`, `adapters/embeddings/base.py`, every vector-db adapter, `api/auth.py`, `services/doctor.py`, `services/traces.py` — and `load_settings` held the **only** `load_dotenv` call in the tree, so deleting the copy would have broken every CLI command, `serve`, the facade and `doctor` on any machine keeping secrets in `.env`. It is therefore kept and made explicit as `export_env`, **defaulting to off**, and passed at the six entry points that go on to build adapters; `config show` alone says `export_env=False`. **The obvious alternative was rejected with a reason**: reading the dotenv into a local mapping the check consults would let `load_settings()` pass while the adapter built two lines later fails, because adapters read `os.environ`, not the mapping — validating against exactly the environment the process will read keeps fail-fast **earlier and stricter**, never later. **This is a breaking change for `pip install fasterrag` consumers** who call `load_settings()` and then build components; it is loud rather than silent, the error naming the dotenv file and `export_env=True`. Verified independently by the orchestrator: `load_settings('config.yaml')` adds **0** names to `os.environ`, **0** dotenv values appear in the error message, and the message names `export_env=True`. **The live probes are now gated on `FASTERRAG_LIVE_PROVIDER_TESTS`** checked *before* the credential, the SDK and the endpoint — a key exported for unrelated work is not consent to spend money — pinned by 12 cases in an **unmarked** gating module that therefore cannot skip in ordinary CI. The nightly's `OPENAI_API_KEY: ci-placeholder` was a latent trap that the opt-in closes. **`CLAUDE.md`'s commands line was false for a second reason and is fixed**: `addopts` excludes only eval/load/soak/chaos, so a bare `pytest` is unit **and integration** (3966 of 4057 collected, 379 integration), while collecting **0** live-provider probes and `-m eval` still collecting 30. — ✅ 2026-11-09

Any CI step or ad-hoc audit relying on per-test contexts must set `COVERAGE_CORE=ctrace` or it measures nothing while reporting cleanly — two passes of the TASK-0335 sweep produced false "0 tests" results before this was found. Worth a line in `docs/testing-strategy.md` and a guard in any future context-based tooling, because it is the vacuity defect class arriving inside the measuring instrument. **Closed, with the failure demonstrated before the fix and the fix demonstrated against it — and the mechanism is worse than this entry recorded.** It is not "empty contexts": `sys.monitoring` stops reporting a line once it has been seen, so under `sysmon` **only the first context to reach a line keeps credit**, and when module import or a session fixture gets there first the sole credited context is `''`. Measured on this tree: `core/chunking/layout.py:49` is entered by **22** tests and credited to **1** under the default core, **22** under the fix — a 22× under-report in a run that exits green. **The fix is mechanical, not a doc line**: `[tool.coverage.run] core = "ctrace"` in `pyproject.toml` (an unknown core raises `ConfigError`, so the pin cannot fail quietly), plus `tests/test_coverage_records_contexts.py`, which asserts behaviour rather than a string — a subprocess probe drives two named contexts through one line and checks the pinned core credits both **and that the default core still does not**, so a future coverage that fixes `sysmon` retires the pin by failing honestly. **Who needed the warning: nobody in CI** — `--cov-context` appears in no script, workflow or config anywhere, so the trap had no gate to be caught by, which is why the fix belongs in repo config. **`COVERAGE_CORE` in the environment still overrides the pin** (verified), so a deliberate override is back in the trap. **Correction, same day**: the third assertion — that the *default* core still loses contexts — failed CI on run 42988401730, because on CI's Python 3.12 `sysmon` credits both contexts while on this machine's 3.14.7 it does not. Whether the trap reproduces depends on the interpreter and the coverage build rather than on this repository, so that assertion now **skips with its reason** instead of failing; the pin and the two assertions that do bind — a core is pinned, and the pinned core credits both — are unchanged. A third instance of TASK-0398, inside a test written about that very class. — ✅ 2026-11-11

Needs a cross-job artifact and a decision about which tests earn the marker. First candidates: the two symlink confinement cases and the live-provider gating tests that deliberately carry no `eval` marker so they cannot skip. **Built.** `@pytest.mark.must_run` is registered in `pyproject.toml` — `--strict-markers` makes an unregistered marker an error, so collection succeeding is the first proof the wiring is real — and carried by **17 node ids**: the two symlink confinement cases, and four functions proving the `FASTERRAG_LIVE_PROVIDER_TESTS` opt-in is checked before the credential plus the counterpart proving an explicit opt-in still admits a probe. **The rule is ran-at-least-once, never ran-everywhere**: the symlink cases legitimately skip on a Windows account without the privilege, so each lane's verdict is kept separate and a finding prints all of them; skipping on *both* is the failure. The roster is collected by a **separate** `--collect-only` pass, because a lane can only report on tests it collected and "absent from every report" must not read as "nothing to check". **Not vacuous**: a missing artifact, an empty reports directory, a roster with zero marked tests, reports holding no outcome, a collect-only document offered as evidence, an unknown report version and a lane naming a test the roster lacks are each a failure, pinned by 17 tests. **Proved on real pytest output** — this account lacks the symlink privilege, so a genuine run produced both cases skipped; the gate exits 0 when one lane ran them and 1 when both skipped. **Unverified:** the `must-run` job has never run on a runner; the artifact round-trip is the one unproven link. — ✅ 2026-11-11
- [x] TASK-0350: **The nightly's regression tolerance is a flap risk.** The measured corpus is 6 documents / 632 tokens and the warmed median is **3.05 ms** (run 42419161973), so the 10% tolerance is **0.3 ms** — inside shared-runner noise. This gate will go red for a neighbouring VM more often than for a regression, and a gate that cries wolf is switched off within the month. Either widen the tolerance with a stated reason, or enlarge the corpus so the measurement is large enough to be a measurement. Both change what every future comparison means, so neither was done unilaterally under TASK-0323. **Closed: tolerance 10% → 125%, derived from measurement rather than chosen.** Seven runs of the identical workload, including **three `workflow_dispatch` runs of the same commit `882a07e` inside ten minutes — 1.98 / 2.87 / 1.39 ms, a 2.07× spread on a tree that did not change by one character — and the 10% gate failed the middle one outright** at "44.9% worse" (run 42973944898, left red deliberately as the evidence). Full sample mean 2.51 ms, sd 0.67, **CV 26.5%**. The gate had already flapped in production on 2026-11-09, where the unusual number was the *baseline*, not the measurement. **The other two options were refused with numbers**: enlarging the corpus cannot work, because within-run repetitions agree to 0.19–2.54% while runs of one commit differ by 106.5% — the dominant term is a multiplicative host-speed difference a larger corpus scales along with the signal; and an absolute floor is the percentage in disguise, since the same-commit pair moved 1.48 ms, which against a 1.39 ms run *is* a 107% tolerance. Reproduce-twice fails because every false red came from an unusually fast baseline. **125% is tighter than the instrument supports**: the spread is multiplicative, so a two-sided 2σ band in log space spans 3.35× = 235%. The residual is published rather than hidden — a 3.07 ms night against the 1.39 ms baseline lands at 120.9%, inside by four points. The gate now detects nothing finer than a **2.25× slowdown**, and the file says it must not be widened again: the answer to a further false red is the instrument (TASK-0387). The runner's CPU model is now printed to the job summary, since the fingerprint records only `x86_64` (TASK-0388). **Unverified:** the edited workflow has never run. — ✅ 2026-11-11

- [x] TASK-0353: **An unclosed `AsyncQdrantClient` reaches the network at interpreter shutdown.** `qdrant_client/async_qdrant_remote.py:231: UserWarning: Failed to obtain server version` appeared in exactly 1 of 6 full unit runs on a frozen tree, printed to stderr *after* the final progress line — outside pytest's warning capture, which is why `-W error:` did not catch it. Some test builds a real client and never closes it. It changed no count. **The exact test was not isolated.** Found 2026-11-08 during TASK-0322. **Closed, and the mechanism was not what this entry assumed.** The test is `tests/unit/test_facade.py::test_doctor_runs_without_starting_the_facade`, isolated by instrumenting `AsyncQdrantRemote.__init__` across the whole unit tier: **exactly one** real client construction in 3,594 tests, via `facade.doctor() → run_doctor → _check_vector_db_reachable → QdrantAdapter.client`. **The client was not unclosed** — that function closes it in a `finally`. `AsyncQdrantRemote.__init__` starts a **daemon thread** that GETs the server version, and `close()` does not join it; a warning raised on that thread belongs to no test, which explains all three oddities at once: why pytest's capture and `-W error:` missed it, why it printed after the final progress line, and why it appeared in 1 of 6 runs. The test now stubs the adapter factory and the Docker/port probes — unstubbed it also shelled out to the daemon, the TASK-0324 machine-dependence defect in a second place — and asserts the adapter was closed. A new autouse guard in `tests/unit/conftest.py` refuses any real `AsyncQdrantRemote`/`QdrantRemote` construction anywhere in the unit tier, naming the offending test and pointing at `tests/integration/`; local `:memory:` clients are untouched. `test_no_network_clients.py` pins that the guard fires, since an untested conftest fixture can be deleted silently. **A trap worth keeping**: the guard must not depend on the `monkeypatch` fixture — an autouse conftest fixture that does forces `monkeypatch` to be built before every module-level autouse fixture, inverting teardown order, which broke 8 tests in `test_tokenizer.py`; recorded as a `# CRITICAL:` block. Full unit tier after the fix: 3628 passed, zero occurrences of the warning. — ✅ 2026-11-09

Cross-test order dependence is therefore untested in either direction: TASK-0322's six identical runs prove the fixed order is clean and nothing more. Given the shared-state writes found alongside it, a one-off randomised run is worth doing before release; if adopted, pin the seed in CI so a failure is reproducible. **Evidence 2026-11-09, not a decision.** The unit tier run with its **top-level directories reversed** gave 3628 passed / 0 failed, identical to forward order at the same tree. This probes **cross-directory leakage only** — collection order within each directory was unchanged — so it is weak evidence and does not settle the question. Nothing was installed.
- [x] TASK-0355: **Make measurement runs state their tree.** `tests/conftest.py` now prints the commit SHA, uncommitted-path count and resolved `fasterrag` import path on every run, including under `-q` (which suppresses the normal header and is the flag every measurement in this repo uses). Make `docs/benchmarks.md` and `docs/verification.md` require that line to be pasted with any recorded number, and make `docs/release-readiness.md` re-derive its counts from a clean tree only. **A number without its tree state is not a measurement** — TASK-0322 cost a session to establish exactly that, and would have been answered from the original two logs in seconds. **Closed.** `docs/benchmarks.md` gains mandatory **ledger rule 7** — an eighth field, `Provenance:`, carrying the four lines `tests/conftest.py` prints verbatim; **an entry missing it is incomplete and may not be cited, exactly as one missing `Commit:` may not**. `docs/verification.md` gains **Tier −1 — State the tree, before any number is recorded**, ahead of Tier 0, with three rules that bite in order: a number without the line is incomplete, `working tree:` must read `clean` for anything compared, and the import path must sit inside the checkout. `docs/release-readiness.md` now requires a clean tree for re-derivation and says why — the 2026-11-08 derivation ran against 29 uncommitted paths, which is why its scores had to be pinned to a HEAD the measurements were not taken on. Each carries TASK-0322 as its stated cause, written as a cost rather than a rule. The three existing ledger entries are **not** retrofitted, and that is stated. **Gap reported, not papered over**: `fasterrag benchmark --ledger` does not emit the field, so rule 7 is a human step (TASK-0389). — ✅ 2026-11-11
- [x] TASK-0356: **Make idempotency record lifetime and the kill switch configurable.** `services/idempotency.py` hard-codes `DEFAULT_TTL_SECONDS` (24 h) and `IN_FLIGHT_LEASE_SECONDS` (15 min), and `IdempotencyStore(enabled=...)` is never set to `false` by anything. The store already takes both as constructor arguments, so this is a settings section plus one line in `api/idempotency.py::_store_for`. Deliberately not done under TASK-0318: `config/` is a declared sensitive folder and that task did not authorize touching it. This is also the flag that would bring TASK-0318 back in line with CLAUDE.md's default-false rule for risky features, which it shipped without and argued for. **Closed. `reliability.idempotency.{enabled,ttl_seconds,in_flight_lease_seconds}`**, with a model validator refusing a lease longer than the lifetime, because an unfinished request outliving a completed one inverts the intent. **`enabled` defaults to `true`, and the deviation from the default-false rule is the decision rather than an oversight.** The rule's exemplars — provisioning, autopilot, the dashboard — all *start doing something*; this one starts nothing, since a request without the header touches no store and writes no byte. Defaulting it off would make a contract published on 2026-11-08 false for every deployment that never read the setting, and the failure is silent: the client's retry executes twice and nothing reports it — a correctness regression wearing a safety default's clothes. **A kill switch that ships already pulled is not a kill switch.** The argument is recorded on the field in `config/schema.py`, in the `api/idempotency.py` docstring and in `config-reference.md`, so the next reader finds it where they hit the decision. Wiring was one line in `_store_for`, as predicted. 3 new tests. **Found and stated, not fixed**: `ttl_seconds` bounds replayability, not disk (TASK-0365). — ✅ 2026-11-09
- [ ] TASK-0357: **Idempotency records are per host, not per deployment.** `IdempotencyStore` writes files under the ingestion journal root, which covers every Gunicorn worker on one machine but not several machines behind a load balancer — there, a retry routed to a second host executes again. Documented as a limit in `docs/api-reference.md` §Idempotency and `docs/reliability.md` rather than hidden. Fix is a shared backend behind the same store interface (the Redis the rate limiter already optionally uses, cf. `security.rate_limit_backend`), or a documented requirement that the journal root be shared storage. **Decide before 1.0, because the header is now public API.** **Partially addressed 2026-11-09: the seam, not the backend.** The answer to "is it substitutable" was **no, for three reasons rather than one**: `IdempotencyStore` was concrete and annotated as such in the middleware; the interface was **synchronous**, with the middleware wrapping each call in `asyncio.to_thread`, so an async Redis client would have had to run an event loop inside a worker thread — the mismatch `core/rate_limit.py` avoided by making `allow`/`close` async, and a quiet deviation from the async-first rule since the store does I/O; and `Reservation` carried a `Path`, leaking filesystem shape across the boundary. All three are closed, reusing `rate_limit.py`'s shape rather than inventing a second. **Proven, not asserted**: a test implements an in-memory backend, assigns it to `app.state.idempotency`, and drives replay, conflict and the record count through the running application with **zero** changes to `api/idempotency.py` and nothing written to disk. **Size of the remaining change, measured**: the middleware touches exactly four symbols; the interface is 25 lines, the file store 186; a Redis backend re-provides three blocking methods and the one hard primitive is native (`SET NX PX` replaces `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` one-for-one); the settings pair, the optional-import/fail-open handling and the `FakeRedis` double all already exist for the rate limiter. Estimate: ~90-line class, one settings pair, one factory branch, one test module, **no caller changes**. **No backend shipped and no decision taken.**
- [x] TASK-0358: **`Journal.find_by_idempotency_key` scans every job record on each call.** It globs `.fasterrag/journal/jobs/*.json` and opens each until it matches, so the deprecated ingest body-field path is O(jobs) per request and degrades as the journal grows. The new header store is O(1), addressing one file by `sha256(key)`. Either index the journal's keys or let this retire with the body field at 1.0 — no work needed if the field goes. **Closed as a decision: do not index. It retires with the body field at 1.0.** Measured first (BENCH-0004): `find_by_idempotency_key` costs **633 ms p50 / 808 ms p95 at 10,000 jobs**, ~63 µs per record, linear — so the "leave it, it's microseconds" reading was **not** available and the answer had to be argued. It is still not an index. **(1)** An index is a second source of truth whose disagreement modes are precisely the failures the field exists to prevent: a miss starts a *second ingest of the same corpus*, a stale hit returns a job id that no longer exists. **(2)** The cost is strictly opt-in to a deprecated field — `create_job` scans nothing when no body key is sent, and the header path is already O(1). **(3)** An index fixes the symptom and leaves the real defect. **Found en route, and it is the larger finding**: `IngestionService.accept` calls the synchronous `Journal.create_job` **inside an `async def`**, so that 633 ms scan runs on the event loop and stalls every other request on the worker — not only the caller using the deprecated field (TASK-0399). The price is recorded in `api-reference.md` so anyone keeping the field sees it. — ✅ 2026-11-11

Blocked on confirming the minimum Ollama server/client version and on a running server to verify against — it raises the deployment floor, so it is a route change rather than an extraction fix, which is why TASK-0312 left it. When it lands, flip `reports_token_usage` to `True` for ollama embeddings and drop its `usage_note`. Ollama is in `LOCAL_PROVIDERS` and priced at zero, so no bill is understated by the gap today. **`/api/embed`'s `prompt_eval_count` behaviour for multi-input requests is doc-only**: the reference shows it on the single-input example and omits it on the multi-input one.
- [ ] TASK-0360: **The mutation child still runs with `cwd=REPOSITORY_ROOT`, so anything resolved relative to the working directory reads the live tree mid-campaign.** `Path.cwd()`, a bare relative path, any future `.env`/`config.yaml` discovery — the residual half of TASK-0303, and precisely the mechanism the `_spec_path()` fallback removed under TASK-0331 was exploiting. Fix is `cwd=workspace`, which changes pytest rootdir and conftest resolution for every target and needs a baseline re-verification of all six, so it wants its own slice.

Measured 2026-11-08: such a target exits 2 with `the unmutated suite exited 2`, so it **refuses rather than scoring** — a capability gap, not a vacuity, which is the right failure direction. Either stage `scripts/` and add it to the child's `PYTHONPATH`, or record the limitation in `docs/testing-strategy.md`. Worth weighing against TASK-0338 and TASK-0342, which are both about defects hiding inside the gate scripts nothing measures.
- [x] TASK-0362: **Wire `--memory-mb` into the nightly `mutation` job and calibrate it.** TASK-0321 shipped the cap but left it unused: the value needs calibrating against the six targets on Linux, because a cap below a legitimate suite's footprint surfaces as `not-run` on the baseline (the guard works; the number is uncalibrated). Separately, distinguish a child killed by a **signal** (`returncode < 0`, e.g. the cgroup OOM killer, which `RLIMIT_AS` cannot convert) from a test failure — both count as `killed` today, so the one pathology the cap cannot catch is also the one it cannot report. `nightly.yml` was deliberately untouched by TASK-0321 because another agent held it. **Closed: `--memory-mb 2048` is wired into the nightly, and the number is measured.** The measurement matters more than the number: **`RLIMIT_AS` bounds address space while `peak_child_rss_bytes` reports resident size, and for these targets the two differ by about 3×** — so a cap read off the 337 MiB the nightly last reported, which is the obvious thing to do, would have turned all six baselines into `not-run`. Measured under WSL Ubuntu-24.04 by running each target's real suite under `ulimit -v`: **all six pass at 896 MiB and above, all six fail below**, and between 640 and 768 MiB the interpreter does not fail cleanly at all — a corrupted `ast` import, a SIGABRT, a SIGINT. The knee is interpreter and imports rather than suite size: `breaker`'s 30 tests fail at the same cap as `chunking`'s 167. 2048 is 2.3× the knee and an upper bound for the hosted runner, since WSL has 16 cores to the runner's 4. **Second half, the sharper one**: `returncode < 0` is now its own outcome — still counted as a kill, still named by signal in JSON, terminal and job summary — so the one memory pathology `RLIMIT_AS` cannot convert, a cgroup OOM kill arriving as SIGKILL from outside, is finally the one it can report. **The calibration produced an instance unprompted**: at 768 MiB the `rate-limit` child aborted with SIGABRT instead of raising MemoryError, which the old code would have recorded as a mutant some test caught. **Unverified:** the cap on `ubuntu-latest`, and a real cgroup OOM kill on a hosted runner. — ✅ 2026-11-11
- [x] TASK-0363: (minor) **`peak_child_rss_bytes` is one cumulative high-water mark across all children.** Per-target peaks would say *which* target got close to the cap, which is the question an operator actually has when calibrating `--memory-mb` (TASK-0362). Per-target `peak_child_rss_bytes` in the artifact, a `Peak MiB` column in the job summary, and both reports now say *"peak child RSS: 337 MiB, first reached in `chunking`"*. The semantics are stated rather than implied: `getrusage(RUSAGE_CHILDREN)` is a high-water mark nothing resets, so the series only rises — the target where it rises is the one that set it, and a target that did not raise it has an upper bound and no reading. Exact per-child figures would need `os.wait4` in place of `subprocess.run` plus a hand-rolled timeout watchdog, which is not minor and was not done. — ✅ 2026-11-11
- [ ] TASK-0364: (security, low) **A third colon in a key entry lands inside the tenant rather than being refused.** `FASTERRAG_API_KEY=k:query:acme:extra` yields `tenant='acme:extra'`, because `remainder.partition(":")` keeps everything after the second colon. It fails **closed** — no `X-Tenant-ID` header can equal `acme:extra`, so the key is simply unusable under multi-tenancy — which makes it a usability trap rather than an escalation, but a silent one: the operator sees a key that authenticates and is then forbidden for every tenant. Observed 2026-11-08 during TASK-0327 and pinned as current behaviour. Decide whether `load_keys` should refuse more than two colons. Related: `;;k:query;;` skips empty entries but still counts them in the error message's index, so `;;k:` reports "entry 3".
- [x] TASK-0365: (reliability) **`FileIdempotencyStore` never reclaims a record whose key does not come back.** Expiry is lazy: `_expired` is consulted only inside `claim`, on a `FileExistsError` for that exact path. A client using a fresh UUID per request — the normal and recommended usage — never returns a key, so **every keyed request leaves a permanent file** under `.fasterrag/journal/idempotency/`. `ttl_seconds` bounds how long a record is *replayable*, not how long its file exists, so the obvious mitigation does not mitigate. Needs a sweeper, or an opportunistic prune of the tenant bucket on write, or the directory grows with keyed traffic forever. Found 2026-11-09 closing TASK-0356; stated honestly in `api-reference.md` pending this id. **Closed, and the design decision was settled by measurement rather than preference** (BENCH-0004). `FileIdempotencyStore.reclaim` removes records expired past a 60-second grace, records that fail their checksum, and `*.tmp` files a crash left behind — **a second leak in the same directory, found while fixing the first**: `write_record` killed between `write_text` and `os.replace` leaves a temporary forever. A pass that removes nothing costs **21.76 ms** over 8,200 records; a pass that removes 2,000 costs **36.6 s**, 97% of it cold `_io.open` by `cProfile` — so this entry's "opportunistic prune on write" would charge the 21.76 ms floor to every keyed request, and an inline sweeper would hand one caller 36 seconds. It therefore runs **beside** a request: `begin` creates a task and does not await it. Measured cost to a *concurrent* request: **+0.07 ms p50, +0.27 ms p95, 16 ms worst of 200** — the tail reported rather than averaged away. **The N-workers problem uses the primitive already here**: the sweep right is one more record taken under `O_CREAT | O_EXCL`, re-taken when its holder dies. **Boundary (TASK-0357):** reclamation lives only on `FileIdempotencyStore`, pinned by `assert not hasattr(IdempotencyStore, "reclaim")`; the one addition to the ABC is `close`, which is resource release and mirrors `RateLimiter.close`. **Stated, not fixed:** `claim` takes an expired record over by rewriting it in place, so a sweep could in principle delete a reservation a takeover wrote microseconds earlier; the grace plus an envelope re-read narrows it to a read and a syscall, and no filesystem offers unlink-if-unchanged. Residual: one duplicate execution of a key abandoned for over a lifetime. **Unverified:** every number is Windows/NTFS and the per-removal cost is dominated by a platform cold-open Linux would not charge. — ✅ 2026-11-11
- [ ] TASK-0366: (performance) **`CostGovernor` memory is O(charges in window), not O(tenants).** `_spent` holds one `(timestamp, tokens)` tuple per charge for the full 24 hours. At 1M queries/day that is ~1M tuples in a now process-lifetime instance, and embedding batches charge into the same deques. The abandoned D9 design bucketed by hour — O(tenants × 24), trading exactness at the window edge for bounded memory. Worth deciding before a high-volume deployment, not after. Found 2026-11-09 under TASK-0337, whose fix is what made the instance long-lived.
- [ ] TASK-0367: **Raise `services/budget.py` coverage, which sits near 40% in the unit tier.** The salvaged `test_cost_governor.py` (TASK-0284) cannot be ported — 7 of its 11 unmined tests target a `TokenLedger` API that was never built — so the task is "raise the coverage", not "port the file"; it can only be mined. 4 tests were already adapted under TASK-0337. **One salvaged assertion contradicts shipped behaviour and must not be re-adopted**: it asserts the streamed path emits no event before a budget refusal, while `stream()` deliberately yields `meta` with the trace id first and `api/query.py` renders a mid-stream error as an SSE `error` event — the shipped design is the better one.

It fails loudly rather than vacuously, so it was not abandoned offering false confidence — but those guards encode a shape of `python-api.md` that no longer exists, which makes repair larger than the gate. Its real signal, worth keeping whatever is decided: **7 public facade members with no row** (`cache`, `create_collection`, `drop_collection`, `embeddings`, `index_lock`, `journal`, `vector_db`) and **2 rows with no member** (`export_archive`, `import_archive`) — the latter because the doc deliberately marks them not yet wrapped, so the test's one-row-one-member premise is itself wrong and must be decided before it is repaired.
- [x] TASK-0369: **The umbrella-id hole stays open for every capability with no registry.** `check_unbuilt_claims.py` (TASK-0309) closes it for vector-DB, embedding and LLM providers by reading the factories, and that is all it can reach. `reliability.md` carried the same defect in a shape no registry decides — "specified but not built (TASK-0243)" for a load harness that had shipped under that still-correctly-open id. Degradation rungs, D-features and test layers are all exposed the same way. Two candidate fixes, neither free: require a denial to cite an id covering **exactly** the thing denied (enforceable only by review), or give umbrella tasks a per-item sub-ledger the gate can read. The hole has produced findings on four separate days. **Closed: one more class is gateable and the rest is not, which is the finding.** Test tiers have a registry — `--strict-markers` means a tier cannot exist outside `[tool.pytest.ini_options].markers`, and a tier *exists* when `tests/<tier>/` holds a module or any test carries its marker: built `{integration, eval, load, chaos}`, unbuilt `{soak}`, exactly the pair the defect turned on. Two design points measured rather than assumed: the vocabulary is **every registered marker, not the `addopts` deselects**, because at `650943b` `addopts` carried no `-m` expression and deriving tiers from it drops `soak` out of the vocabulary on the exact tree that was wrong about soak's neighbour; and markers are read with `ast`, never grepped, because the substring version **was fooled by this gate's own test**, which writes `"@pytest.mark.soak"` inside a string fixture. **Degradation rungs and D-features stay ungated, deliberately**: `hybrid_only` is a bare module constant, `extractive` is not even that, `cache_only` appears nowhere in `src/`, so a grep decides a rung by whether a docstring happens to spell it — `cross_encoder.py`'s does — and a wrong *yes* fails an honest sentence, which is how a gate gets switched off. **Both candidate fixes rejected with reasons**: a per-item sub-ledger is a second document asserting things about the tree with nothing checking it (the umbrella defect moved down one level), and anything able to check it could read the tree instead; the discipline rule is enforceable only by review, which is a comment and not a control. The buildable route is TASK-0394. — ✅ 2026-11-11
