Left unchanged by the TASK-0309 sweep on the argument that a derivation stamped at a commit is a record like a CHANGELOG entry — but **nothing in the document says that**, while `check_claims.py` exempts `CHANGELOG.md` explicitly and exempts this implicitly-never. A reader has no way to tell a stale claim from a dated one. **Closed: the document is live, every dated derivation inside it is frozen.** The evidence is in the document — §1.1 says "Scored against `main` at `650943b`", and a derivation that names its commit has already declared itself a snapshot. Timing verified: `650943b` is 2026-11-08 00:19 +0530 and Weaviate landed at 01:21 — **62 minutes later**, so "4 of 6" was true when written. A standing note now names which sections are which, and records that **`check_claims.py` keeps scanning this file** rather than gaining a CHANGELOG-style exemption, which would silence the gate over 196 lines; corrections move into a new live §0 and the frozen text is never edited. §0 is re-derived under the new clean-tree rule from the **committed object** at `882a07e`, the worktree being dirty: adapters are **5 of 6, only `pinecone` refused**. §0 adjusts no score — that is a release-decision act needing maintainer authorization, and a list of corrections is not a derivation. — ✅ 2026-11-11
- [x] TASK-0371: **README's capability table denies by column header, which no gate can read.** Its "not built" column lists capabilities with **no denial verb in any cell** — the denial lives in the header, while `check_claims.py` and `check_unbuilt_claims.py` both need a denial phrasing and both read the row cell-by-cell. This is how "Milvus/Weaviate/Pinecone adapters" and "load and soak test layers" sat in that column after they had shipped. Either put the denial in the cells, or teach the table-row splitter to inherit a column header — the second is the general fix and the harder one. **Closed with the general fix, not the cheap one.** The splitter now inherits a denial from a column header, and from a prose lead-in label too, since `**Specified but not built** (TASK-0243): load …` is a column header with a colon. Two things made it precise. **The corpus parse was missing the only table that matters** — README's sits in a blockquote and `_TABLE_ROW` anchors on `^\s*\|`, so stripping the quote prefix is the difference between **152 tables and 151**, the missing one being the *only denying header in the repository*. And the naive version has a live false positive: README's own `a Milvus or Weaviate contract run against a real server` sits in that column and is **entirely true**, so a rule that merely looks for a registered name inside an inherited-denial cell fails the most careful sentence on the page. The rule therefore decides only when **the item IS the thing** — an enumeration of known names, one optional count before each, one optional noun after the last, nothing else. **Measured over four trees: 574 tables, 19 denied-column items, 1,177 tier clause-mentions, 3 findings, 0 false positives** — and all three are on `650943b`, *the tree the previous sweep produced and every gate passed*. - [x] TASK-0372: **The `swallow` rule's probe exemption covers only a `pass` arm on a `try`/`except`/`else`, not a `continue`/`break` arm on a probing `try`.** That gap is the entire reason the ratchet is no longer empty: `mutation_run.py:536` is structurally the same thing as an `else`-arm probe — the `try` binds a name the code after it reads, and the handler is the false branch — but takes `continue` because it sits in a loop. A principled widening, **measured over `src/` and `scripts/` before it ships**, would let the ratchet go back to empty without weakening the rule. Weigh against simply leaving the entry: a one-line measured ratchet is honest, and a wrong exemption is permanent. **Closed: the widening was measured and refused, and the ratchet entry stays.** The only principled form is "the `try` binds a name a later statement reads, and the handler is trivial". Measured over `src/` and `scripts/`: it exempts **exactly one site today** — `mutation_run.py:536`, the ratchet's own outcome — while matching **134** `try` statements whose handler does real work, **14** of them inside a loop where `continue` is already legal. As predicted, the usual zero-false-positive evidence does not exist, so those 14 are the argument: `config/loader.py:135` (a `--set` value silently dropped, weakening the fail-fast config contract), `cli/sources.py:41` (a user-named source silently not ingested), `workers/cpu_pool.py:223` and `embed_pool.py:254` (a work item silently lost) — **and `check_error_taxonomy.py:534`, the gate's own `unreadable` handler**, so the widening would conceal a revert of the rule TASK-0342 added to stop exactly that. **Proved, not asserted**: the widening was implemented in a scratch copy and run against a gate whose `unreadable` rule had been reverted to `except SyntaxError: continue` — the shipped gate reports the swallow, the widened gate reports nothing. The shape cannot separate "the exception is the answer" from "the exception is a failure we are hiding", because a loop skipping a bad item and a loop skipping a failed one are the same tree; a `try`/`except`/`else` differs in kind, since writing the `else` names the success path deliberately and nobody writes one by accident. So the one-line ratchet entry stays — keyed by path, still failing the file's second swallow, still TASK-0338's canary, and revertible, where a wrong exemption would be permanent and silent. **Pinned by opposite polarity**: with the widening applied, the new refusal test fails *and* `test_every_ratchet_entry_still_describes_the_real_tree` fails, because the widening makes the entry vacuous. — ✅ 2026-11-11
- [x] TASK-0373: **The `unreadable` rule is implemented three times, near-identically, in three gate scripts.** A shared `scripts/_gatelib.py` is the obvious fix and is probably the wrong one: these scripts are deliberately standalone and stdlib-only, because they run before install and `mutation_run.py`'s `-o pythonpath=` trick depends on it (TASK-0361). Record the duplication as deliberate, or find a sharing mechanism that survives both constraints. **Closed: the stated constraint is false, and the duplication stays for a different, measured reason.** Checked with a throwaway sibling pair rather than taken on faith. A sibling import resolves in **both real uses** — run as a script from any working directory, and under pytest's ini `pythonpath` — and under `mutation_run.py`'s `-o pythonpath=` it fails **identically to `import check_error_taxonomy` itself**, which already raises `ModuleNotFoundError` there. TASK-0361's gap is prior to and independent of the sibling question. **The second premise is wrong too**: the three are not near-identical — three different payloads, and they disagree on whether a partial read is kept or discarded, while `check_task_ids` does not emit a `Finding` at all. If a pathological document is ever seen to flood the log, the `self._warned` pattern is the fix. **Closed, and it settles more cleanly than "below the threshold".** `_heading_level`'s warning **cannot be reached from document content at all**: markdown-it's `heading_open` token always carries `h1`–`h6`, so `int(token.tag[1:])` raises only via a plugin or custom renderer. - [ ] TASK-0376: **Extend the unit-tier network guard beyond Qdrant's two remote classes.** TASK-0353's autouse guard refuses a real `AsyncQdrantRemote`/`QdrantRemote`, but `run_doctor` still reaches the Docker daemon, `nvidia-smi` and local ports from unit tests, and `_check_gpu` makes a unit result machine-dependent in its `detail`. That is the TASK-0324 class of machine-dependence: a unit test whose outcome depends on the box it runs on. A broader socket/subprocess guard would close it; the cost is finding the legitimate exceptions first.
- [x] TASK-0377: (security, blocks turning `tenant_scoped_retrieval` on) **No backfill exists for the `tenant` payload field.** Chunks ingested before `security.multi_tenancy` was enabled carry no such field, and an absent field never matches a filter, so the flag over an unbackfilled corpus returns **empty results, not wrong ones** — fail-closed but silent, which is harder to diagnose than a 404. The adapter contract already has a metadata-only `PointUpdate`; nothing uses it for this. Needs a `fasterrag index backfill-tenant`-shaped command or an admin endpoint, plus a documented ordering against TASK-0378. **Built as a CLI pair, deliberately not an endpoint.** `fasterrag index tenants [NAME]` counts every collection's points by `tenant` tag and names the tenants present — read-only discovery over the `iterate_points` traversal TASK-0079 added rather than a second walk. `fasterrag index backfill-tenant NAME --tenant ID` writes the tag through the metadata-only `PointUpdate`, **dry-running by default**; an applied run leaving points pending exits 1. **Idempotent by predicate rather than bookkeeping**: a point is selected only when it carries no tag, which is the negation of the state the write produces, so a completed run is a fixpoint and a run killed halfway is finished by running it again — no checkpoint file, because a cursor that can go stale is a second thing to get right. Both crash orders are pinned. **It refuses to relabel**: a collection holding another tenant's points is `CONFLICT` in dry run as well as under `--apply`, because its untagged points cannot be attributed to either tenant — the defect fixed once in `chunk_payload`. The audit runs in full before the first write, costing a second traversal, because a foreign tenant found halfway would leave half a collection relabelled. **Not an endpoint**: every admin route is tenant-scoped, and choosing the owner of a point that has none is not a decision a request-scoped credential should make. `docs/security.md` §3 gained step 0 and a real step 1, plus the ordering — **index after the backfill, not before**. **Not verified:** no live vector database executed a `PointUpdate`. — ✅ 2026-11-11
- [ ] TASK-0378: (performance, touches `adapters/`) **Nothing in the repo creates a backend payload index, and `CollectionSpec` has no field for one.** With `tenant_scoped_retrieval` on, both retrieval legs pay an **unindexed** filter on every query, which on a large collection is the worst case rather than the cheap one; filtered HNSW is also strictly dearer than unfiltered search over a smaller graph, because the walk must traverse further to find `limit` surviving neighbours. Decide whether payload-index declaration joins the vendor-neutral `CollectionSpec` — **this is the one part of TASK-0339's option (b) that touches the vendor-isolation boundary**, so it wants its own authorization. **Scoped 2026-11-11 without implementation; `adapters/` untouched. This entry's premise needs correcting: the tree is not uniformly unindexed.** Verified by reading all five adapters: **pgvector already creates `GIN (payload jsonb_path_ops)` per collection** (`pgvector.py:654`) and translates equality to `payload @> …` (`:548`), so its tenant filter is indexed today; Weaviate flattens scalar payload keys into real properties, so `tenant` is probably filterable via auto-schema, undeclared and unverified. **Qdrant and Milvus are the real gap** — Qdrant needs one idempotent `create_payload_index(..., is_tenant=True)`, while Milvus scans its JSON payload column **by its own adapter docstring's admission** (`milvus.py:29`) and, with a schema fixed at creation, cannot gain one on an existing collection without a blue/green reindex. Chroma has nothing declarable. **A `CollectionSpec` field alone is close to useless**, since it would only help collections created afterwards while TASK-0377 exists precisely because the corpus already exists: the minimum honest shape is a declaration plus an idempotent `ensure_payload_indexes` returning per-field state, so Chroma can report `unsupported` and Milvus `requires_rebuild` rather than silently no-opping. Known costs: an abstract method on six adapters, a contract case across five live backends, `CollectionInfo` having to report it or drift cannot see it, **lockfile drift on every existing collection** when the config hash changes, an archive manifest carrying no index declaration so a restore comes back unindexed, and `_require_compatible` having to reconcile rather than refuse.
- [ ] TASK-0379: (security) **Enabling `tenant_scoped_retrieval` does not invalidate semantic-cache entries written while it was off.** Those entries were produced by cross-tenant retrieval and are tagged with the tenant that asked, so a stale hit still carries content that was already leaked to that tenant — turning the flag on does not re-derive them. Documented as "clear the cache when enabling"; a transition that clears or re-tags automatically would be better than an instruction a reader can miss.
- [ ] TASK-0380: (API surface) **The Python facade and the CLI build retrieval with no credential, so with `tenant_scoped_retrieval` on they raise `TENANT_FORBIDDEN`** — the flag is API-only. `FasterRag.ingest` already takes a `tenant`; `FasterRag.query`/`retrieve` and the CLI do not. Decide whether they gain one, which changes the documented `python-api.md` surface. Reasoned from the code path (`facade.py` and three `RetrievalService` call sites pass no tenant), **not executed**.
- [ ] TASK-0381: (security) **The ingest path does not apply `scoped_name`, so a collection created through `/v1/collections` as `acme__docs` is ingested into and queried as `docs`.** Whether or not TASK-0339 chooses name-scoping for retrieval, ingest and `/v1/collections` disagreeing about where a collection lives is a defect on its own — it is why two tenancy models coexist, and why the shared unscoped collection answered for everybody in the executed proof.
- [x] TASK-0382: **The nightly mutation job reports survivors as a warning and stays green.** Run 42419161973 succeeded with 11 survivors of 60. A survivor ratchet — fail when more survive than the measured baseline — would close it. The idea arrived with `check_mutation_ratchet.py`, salvaged under TASK-0284 and discarded because it was built for `mutmut` and reads `mutants/mutmut-cicd-stats.json`, while this project uses its own AST runner; the ceiling must be re-derived against `scripts/mutation_run.py`'s own artifact, which TASK-0321 now writes incrementally with per-target counts. **Closed. The ratchet ships as `--check-ratchet` inside `scripts/mutation_run.py`, not as a new `check_*.py`**, so it reuses `decide()`, `RunReport` and the artifact schema instead of re-deriving them — a second parser of a format it does not own is what made the salvaged script worth discarding twice. Four decisions. **Per-target, not total**: a total of 11 is satisfied by `chunking` 5→7 behind `identity` 2→0, and only a per-target ceiling names the target. **Counts, not identities**: the seeded sample is redrawn whenever a target file changes — `chunking` generated 234 mutants on 11-07 and 238 on the three nights after, a different ten mutants, five surviving both times, so the count is stable where the identity is not. **Timeouts and signals get a second ceiling, at zero**: TASK-0321 keeps a timeout inside `killed`, so a ratchet reading only `survived` inherits a free pass for the mutant that turned a bounded loop unbounded — perfect kill count, suite proving nothing. **Lowering is mechanical, raising is a hand edit**: a run under its ceiling prints the replacement block to paste, a run over it prints **no block at all**, because a tool that hands back a rubber stamp is one. **The ceiling is measured, not chosen** — four nights across three commits report the identical split (retrieval 1, chunking 5, identity 2, breaker 1, regression 0, rate-limit 2 of 60, zero timeouts) — so it carries no slack. Each entry records the sample it was measured on, because without that `--budget 2` satisfies the ratchet for free. Vacuity-guarded by reusing `decide()`: fourteen distinct conditions exit 2. **Proved against the genuine artifact**, not only synthesised ones. — ✅ 2026-11-11

Replace the Windows estimate with the runner number, since that is the environment the step actually runs in. **Closed 2026-11-11.** Re-measured independently before replacing it: **971 / 1016 / 960 ms** locally, confirming the 1.67-1.83 s figure had drifted. - [ ] TASK-0385: (security) **A collection holding two tenants' chunks plus untagged ones has no remedy in the repo.** `index backfill-tenant` correctly refuses it, and `--overwrite` is only right when the whole collection is one tenant's — but **the shared unscoped collection TASK-0339 observed is exactly this case**, and splitting it (export → partition by payload → import under scoped names) is a manual procedure that does not exist. - [ ] TASK-0386: (minor) **The tenant backfill audits the whole collection twice under `--apply`** — once to refuse foreign tenants before writing, once to write. A single-pass mode that buffers updates and commits only if no foreign tag was seen, or a `--trust-audit` flag reusing a prior `index tenants` run, would remove it — neither worth building before anyone has run this against a real backend.
- [ ] TASK-0387: (performance, the real fix behind TASK-0350) **Make the nightly comparison host-paired.** TASK-0350 measured a **2.07× spread on one commit in twenty minutes**, so the night-over-night comparison is not salvageable by any tolerance and 125% detects only a 2.25× slowdown. Benchmark the working tree **and a fixed reference commit in the same job on the same VM** so host speed cancels, then return the tolerance to single digits — the within-run term is under 3%. **Blocked on a way to verify a workflow change before committing it**: `workflow_dispatch` runs the file already on the ref, so an unverifiable rewrite of the repository's only measurement job cannot be reviewed the way code is.

- [ ] TASK-0389: **Make `fasterrag benchmark --ledger` emit the `Provenance` field.** `benchmarks.md` rule 7 (TASK-0355) requires commit, working-tree state and import path on every entry, and an entry without it may not be cited; the generator captures the hardware fingerprint and commit hash only, so rule 7 is a manual step until this lands — which is exactly the shape of rule that decays.
- [ ] TASK-0390: **Establish whether the nightly's 2× spread is CPU generation or co-tenant contention.** It decides how much a paired measurement (TASK-0387) can recover: pairing cancels the first completely and the second only partly. The CPU-model line `nightly.yml` now writes to the job summary is the data source; a week of nights answers it. Stated as "not established" in `docs/performance.md` rather than guessed.
- [ ] TASK-0391: **`semantic.py::_sentences`'s `if boundary > cursor` false arm is dead.** `_SENTENCE_END.finditer` yields non-overlapping matches, so `match.end()` strictly increases and `cursor` only ever takes a previous boundary; the arc is never taken in the suite (branch census, 2026-11-11). Same class as the guard removed under TASK-0346: prove and remove, or find the input.
- [ ] TASK-0392: **`pooling.py::_windows`'s `while start < total` exit arc is never taken.** The loop always leaves through `if end == total: break`, and `total == 0` is refused upstream. Either the guard is dead or the upstream refusal is what makes it dead — worth deciding which, since the second case means the guard documents a contract rather than handling a case.
- [ ] TASK-0393: **`LayoutChunker` drops every character before its first block.** `_pack_blocks` tiles from `blocks[0].start`, so a `ParsedDocument` whose first block starts at 8 chunks as `[(8, 30)]` for a 30-character document (measured 2026-11-11) — **invariant 1 broken**, in `_pack_blocks` rather than in `_merge_blank`. Unreachable through the shipped parsers, since the builder's first block starts at 0; reachable from the documented plugin surface. Related to but distinct from TASK-0307.
- [ ] TASK-0394: **Make the degradation ladder an enum so a rung denial becomes decidable.** TASK-0369 closed the umbrella hole for providers and test tiers because each has a registry; the ladder has none. `services/querying.py` carries `mode: str` with two loose constants, `extractive` is not a constant anywhere, and `cache_only` appears nowhere in `src/` — so "the `cache_only` rung is specified but not built (TASK-0159)" is true today and **nothing can tell when it stops being true**, while a grep for a rung name would answer *yes* off `cross_encoder.py`'s docstring. An enum, or a rung→implementation mapping the ladder dispatches through so a rung cannot ship without registering, makes `check_unbuilt_claims.py` decide the class in about ten lines. Touches `services/`, so it needs slice authorization; the gate side is trivial once it lands.
- [ ] TASK-0395: (minor, record the judgement either way) **Decide whether to report denials that rest on an umbrella id at all.** TASK-0369 concluded the ledger-discipline rule is review-only, but one mechanical half exists: a gate could list every id cited by denials of **two or more distinct subjects** and call it an umbrella citation needing a human. It decides nothing and would be advisory output — which is exactly why it may be noise that trains people to ignore the step. Deliberately not built on 2026-11-11; record the decision so it is not re-litigated.
- [ ] TASK-0396: **Dispatch the vector-backend contract job once, then promote it into `ci.yml`'s `integration` job.** The stack is committed (`deploy/compose.test-backends.yml`) and the job block is written out in `docs/testing-strategy.md` §3.1; what is missing is a run. Needs a JUnit reader that fails on a **skipped** case, an empty report or no report — because pytest exits 0 when everything skips, and a job reading only the exit code reproduces TASK-0352 one level up. Sequence: run it by hand, fix what the adapters do wrong on first contact with a real server, then add the job in the same commit as the ledger entry saying it runs.

- [ ] TASK-0398: (release-blocking for reproducibility) **The dev environment, `uv.lock`, and CI are three different resolutions, and nothing makes any two of them agree.** The 2026-11-08 red `main` was `mypy` passing locally and failing in CI because CI had `httpx2` and the dev venv had only `httpx`, and `starlette.testclient` binds whichever is present; the instance was fixed with a structural `Protocol`, the class was not. **Measured 2026-11-11: `uv.lock` is validated by `uv lock --check` and audited daily by `security.yml`, and installed from by nothing** — every CI job runs `pip install -e ".[dev]"`, an unpinned resolve at job time, with extras differing per job. **81 of 170 installed packages differ in version from the lock**, including `mypy` 2.3.0 / 2.4.0 / 2.4.1 and `ruff` 0.16.2 / 0.17.3 / 0.17.4 (venv / lock / fresh resolve), and the interpreter is **3.14.7 here and 3.12 in every CI job**. Not every floating pin is live: `ruff==0.17.3` was run in isolation and passes on today's tree; `mypy>=1.11` floating across a major version is the largest unmeasured hazard. **The gate that closes this is one change — install from the lockfile (`uv sync --locked`)** — and it needs a maintainer, because it trades "upstream breakage reds the next push" for "upstream breakage appears when someone runs `uv lock --upgrade`", needs extra-scoped lock groups so CI does not install CUDA, and needs the dev venv rebuilt from the lock and kept rebuilt, which no gate can enforce on a machine CI never sees. **Two half-gates are explicitly rejected**: a step that diffs versions and warns is the TASK-0314/0382 shape that stays green and is read by nobody, and a step that fails on drift while CI still resolves unpinned would red `main` on every upstream release.
- [ ] TASK-0399: (reliability) **`IngestionService.accept` does blocking journal I/O on the event loop.** `services/ingestion.py:103` calls the synchronous `Journal.create_job` from inside an `async def`, so every file read and write it performs runs on the loop and stalls every other request on that Uvicorn worker. Measured at **633 ms p50 at 10,000 jobs** when the deprecated `idempotency_key` body field triggers `find_by_idempotency_key` (BENCH-0004), but **the defect is not confined to that path**: `save_job` writes on the loop for *every* ingest submission, with or without a key, and the same shape is in `load_job`, `record_document`, `remember_content` and `known_content` wherever an async caller reaches them. CLAUDE.md requires all I/O paths to be `async`. Fix is an `asyncio.to_thread` boundary at the async call sites, or an async journal facade — not an index, which TASK-0358 declined. **Survives the removal of the body field at 1.0**, which is why it is filed separately.
- [x] TASK-0400: (docs, minor) **`core/cache/store.py`'s module docstring advertises a `sweep` the interface does not have.** It opens "One narrow interface — get, set, delete, sweep — over the backends"; `CacheStore` declares no `sweep` and no backend implements one. A docstring naming a method that does not exist is the doc-truth defect class one level down, inside code rather than documentation. Either add the method or delete the word. Found 2026-11-11 while looking for an existing sweep precedent. **Closed 2026-11-11, and it was wrong in both directions.** The docstring said "get, set, delete, sweep"; `CacheStore` declares `get`, `set`, `delete`, `clear`, `items` and `close` — so it named one method that does not exist **and omitted three that do**. The only `sweep` in `src/` is `autopilot.sweep_index_time`, unrelated. Corrected to name the real interface rather than adding the method, since nothing calls one and the three backends disagree about expiry by design — which the rest of that docstring explains. — ✅ 2026-11-11
- [ ] TASK-0401: **`rebuild-playbook.md` T12 describes a third `gate_ran: false` state that does not exist on `main`.** Confirmed 2026-11-11 by the TASK-0284 re-audit: `gate_ran` appears nowhere in `services/regression.py` and `record_baseline` nowhere in `src/`; the two commits implementing it (`662ee49`, `8b44b27`) live only on the abandoned branch `worktree-agent-ad8e44f0b262aa083`, and `git merge-base --is-ancestor` confirms neither is on main. So a trap-registry entry whose purpose is to stop someone rebuilding the system wrongly currently documents behaviour the system does not have. Either land the branch's work or correct the playbook — and note that the branch also carries the `--record-baseline` CLI surface, so landing it is a feature decision, not a doc fix.
- [ ] TASK-0191: (maintainer review) `VectorDBAdapter` gained `delete_snapshot` under TASK-0138. Backup retention cannot reclaim space without it: a snapshot lives inside the backend, so pruning a set without deleting its snapshot leaves the snapshot orphaned and invisible to every manifest. Qdrant has `delete_snapshot`; a backend without one could not satisfy the contract. Same boundary question as TASK-0126, TASK-0133, and TASK-0182 — confirm or revert

### S12 — Autopilot v1

- [x] TASK-0086: Implement suggest-only eval-driven auto-tuning with measured deltas; assert zero writes to config.yaml (D6) — `fasterrag autopilot run` searches query-time parameters against a golden set, writes `autopilot-suggestion.yaml`, and verifies config.yaml is byte-identical afterwards; searching index-time parameters is TASK-0145 — ✅ 2026-08-02
- [x] TASK-0144: D6's acceptance test — "on a fixture corpus with a known-better config, autopilot's suggestion matches or beats it" — now verified against a real corpus, not only by unit test. On `tests/eval/datasets/policies` the known-better configuration is BM25-weighted retrieval (nDCG@5 0.9308, MRR 0.9062, measured independently) because the discriminators are literal tokens — a year, a region, a role — that BM25 matches exactly and dense embeddings blur. Started from the default 1.0/1.0 weights, autopilot evaluated 7 candidates in 33 s and suggested `bm25_weight=1.0, dense_weight=0.5` at **nDCG 0.9308 / MRR 0.9062 — matching the known-better score exactly**, reported as `ndcg +0.0231, mrr +0.0312`. `config.yaml` was confirmed byte-unmodified afterwards and the suggestion was written to a separate file marked NOT APPLIED. Recall stayed at 1.0000 across all seven trials, which is exactly why the handbook fixture could not run this test — ✅ 2026-08-03
- [x] TASK-0145: Autopilot searched query-time parameters only; chunk size, overlap and the embedding model change how the index is *built*, so each candidate needs a rebuild — ✅ 2026-11-04 (built as a **separate two-phase operation**, not folded into `tune()`: one budget cannot span evaluations costing a query sweep and candidates costing a full re-chunk and re-embed without the time-box quietly ceasing to mean anything. `plan_index_sweep` prices every candidate with D9's own estimator — reused, so the figure matches `fasterrag estimate` — and reports chunks, tokens, cost and the scratch collection names **before** anything is built; `sweep_index_time` then refuses unless `approved_rebuilds` covers the plan, **defaulting to 0**, and refuses partial approval rather than truncating the grid, since 'approved 2 of 5' would redefine *approved* as *started*. Scratch collections are named unlike `reindex.green_name` so one can never be mistaken for a green build, are recorded **before** the build starts and dropped in a `finally`, and a drop that itself fails is logged and returned as `leaked` rather than swallowed. **A trap found and refused up front**: an `embeddings.model` candidate scored with the injected router would embed *queries* with the baseline model and report the mismatch as a quality number, so it is refused without a `router_factory` before any rebuild runs. 29 tests, 31 mutations, no survivors — two initially survived and the tests were strengthened rather than the table adjusted. **Never executed end to end**: no Docker here, so no scratch collection has been created or dropped against a real backend and no candidate has been scored on an index built for it; the pricing half does run for real. **Open**: no CLI or API surface, and the config key to add is `autopilot.max_index_sweep_rebuilds: Annotated[int, Field(ge=0, le=20)] = 0`)

### S13 — Langfuse + Grafana integrations

- [x] TASK-0044: Implement Grafana auto-provisioning (provisioning-as-code datasources/dashboards, editable:false, allowUiUpdates:false, 30 s reload) — verified end to end: fasterRag → Prometheus → Grafana's provisioned datasource returned real series — ✅ 2026-08-02
- [x] TASK-0154: The provisioned dashboard rendered six empty panels. Three causes, all fixed: five catalogue metrics (`queue_depth`, `dlq_depth`, `ingest_documents_total`, `ingest_throughput`, `cost_usd_total`) were declared but written by nothing; the Prometheus host port and container port had been collapsed into one constant, binding 9099:9099 where nothing listens; and no panel carried a `legendFormat`, so each legend rendered the whole label set and truncated before the only label that varied. Verified live: ingest and query over HTTP moved every wired series, and Grafana's datasource proxy returned them — ✅ 2026-08-02

A provisioning failure is logged and swallowed: refusing to serve queries because a dashboard would not start inverts the dependency — ✅ 2026-08-03
- [x] TASK-0147: Grafana provisioning is not doctor-gated. The Qdrant provisioner checks `fasterrag doctor` before mutating anything; the Grafana one should use the same gate so a port conflict is reported with a fix rather than as a container that fails to start — `require_provisioning_gate` extracted from the Qdrant provisioner and called by Grafana before any manifest is written — ✅ 2026-08-03
- [x] TASK-0148: `fasterrag_circuit_state` is the one catalogue metric nothing writes, because no circuit breaker exists — only its configuration under `reliability.circuit_breaker`. The dashboard's circuit panel is empty by design until the breaker in `docs/reliability.md` §3 is implemented, and says so in its panel description; `test_no_catalogue_metric_is_declared_and_never_written` pins this as the only exemption — ✅ 2026-08-06 (`core/breaker.py`, three states per the spec, wired into the embedding pool's retry loop. `reliability.circuit_breaker.enabled` **defaults to true**, so until now the shipped default advertised a breaker that did not exist. Measured against a stub provider that is permanently down, ingesting 40 chunks: **80 provider attempts and 60 retries without it, 5 attempts and 3 retries with it** — a 16× reduction in calls to a provider already known dead, with all 40 chunks still dead-lettering correctly so nothing is silently lost. A demonstration of behaviour against a stub, not a benchmark-ledger entry. Only *retryable* failures count, because a bad API key fails identically forever and counting it would present a permanent misconfiguration as an intermittent outage. **A real bug was caught by its own test**: `record_failure` read the raw state field while the half-open transition is lazy on the property, so a failed probe on a breaker nothing had observed left `_opened_at` stale and would half-open again on the very next call — one probe admitted per call instead of per timeout. The checked-in flow calls `check()` first and would have hidden it. `KNOWN_UNWRITTEN` is now empty: every catalogue metric has a write site. Does not close TASK-0165, which also covers the `cache_only` rung (TASK-0159))
- [x] TASK-0149: Langfuse provisioning is not doctor-gated either, and needs the same gate as TASK-0147. Its eight published ports are the widest surface any toggle claims, and a conflict currently surfaces as a Compose failure after some containers have already started — gated before any secret is generated, since a conflict discovered mid-`compose up` leaves some of the eight containers running and some not — ✅ 2026-08-03
- [x] TASK-0150: `observability.langfuse: true` does not trigger provisioning at startup — `fasterrag provision langfuse` does. Same gap as TASK-0146 on the Grafana side, and both should be fixed by the same config-driven path — same startup path as TASK-0146, both toggles handled together — ✅ 2026-08-03
- [x] TASK-0151: Export traces to the provisioned Langfuse — `observability/langfuse_export.py` maps a `Trace` onto Langfuse's ingestion API and the trace store ships every trace it writes. The local copy is still authoritative: replay and `GET /v1/traces/{id}` read it, so an incident investigation never depends on the observability stack being healthy. Export is fire-and-forget with the task retained until it completes (asyncio holds only a weak reference, so an unretained task can vanish mid-flight), and a failure is logged and dropped because the query was answered long before it ran. The generation stage is sent as a `generation-create` rather than a `span-create`, because Langfuse derives model usage and cost only from that type. **Verified against a real local HTTP server** — path `/api/public/ingestion`, Basic auth header, 3-event batch, and 207/401/unreachable all handled — but **not yet against a live Langfuse**: Docker Desktop was down when this landed. That last mile is TASK-0178 — ✅ 2026-08-03
